Since 2 March 2026, there has been an ongoing war in Lebanon between Israel and the militant group [Hezbollah](13919.md), with Israel invading parts of Lebanon. It is a resumption of major fighting in the [Hezbollah–Israel conflict](75005425.md), and part of the wider [conflict in the Middle East](75895273.md). The war has precipitated a [humanitarian crisis](1251700.md), resulted in the deaths of more than 4,000 people from Israeli strikes in Lebanon, and seen the [forced displacement](477535.md) of over 1 million.

Hezbollah attacked Israel on October 8, 2023, one day after [Hamas](13913.md)' [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) at the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), which led to the [2024 Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon). After an Israel-Lebanon ceasefire agreement in November 2024, Israel still conducted airstrikes, saying that Hezbollah breached ceasefire conditions. Hezbollah launched strikes on Israel on 2 March 2026, in response to Israel and the United States launching [a war against Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_War), Hezbollah's main backer, and [assassinating](82537558.md) its supreme leader. Israel resumed airstrikes across Lebanon on a larger scale and, on March 16, began ground operations in southern Lebanon. The main bridges on the Litani River were blown up by Israel to cut off the south from the rest of Lebanon; [Bint Jbeil](6124393.md) became a site of [intense ground combat](82919076.md). Israel announced it would demolish Lebanese border villages and occupy Lebanon up to the Litani. Lebanon moved to ban Hezbollah's military activities; it also condemned Israeli attacks on Lebanese civilians. [Iran](14653.md) called for an end to Israeli attacks in Lebanon as a condition for a ceasefire in the wider war.

After a [ceasefire](82888027.md) halted the Iran war, Hezbollah halted its attacks. Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) said the ceasefire did not apply to Lebanon, contradicting Iran and the Pakistani mediator, and launched the [largest airstrikes of the war](82903314.md). On 16 April, Israel and Lebanon agreed to a 10-day [ceasefire](82970933.md), later extended more than once. On 3 June, Israel and Lebanon reached an agreement, facilitated by the US. On June 4, Hezbollah rejected the Lebanon-Israel deal and demanded a full ceasefire and a full Israeli withdrawal from Lebanon. Speaker of Lebanese Parliament [Nabih Berri](592775.md) suggested that Hezbollah and Israel both withdraw from south of the Litani.&#x20;

On 15 June, the [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md) was agreed between the US and Iran, including a ceasefire in Lebanon. Israeli forces remained in occupied areas of Lebanon. On 19 June, U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) announced an Israel-Hezbollah truce entering into force at 4 p.m. local time. On 26 June, US secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) announced a framework deal between Israel and Lebanon for "lasting peace and security". The agreement includes full disarmament of Hezbollah; it was rejected by Hezbollah. On 30 June, Iran announced that it, along with the US and Lebanon, has reached an agreement to establish a committee to oversee the conclusion of the war. On 15 July, the [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) reported that Israel has agreed to withdraw from pilot zones in southern Lebanon. On 20 July, the US announced that Israel handed over sections of the buffer zone occupied by it in southern Lebanon to the [Lebanese government](78886120.md) as per its U.S.-mediated deal with Beirut. [Lebanon's army](17779.md) began deploying in [Zawtar al-Gharbiyeh](78327596.md) on July 21, after an Israeli withdrawal in line with the agreement.

## Background

Main articles: [Hezbollah–Israel conflict (2023–present)](75005425.md), [2024 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_War), and [2026 Iran war](82533582.md)

In the aftermath of [the outbreak of](74998317.md) the [Gaza war](74998519.md) in October 2023, Israel and the Lebanese [Shia](26961.md) militia [Hezbollah](13919.md) have [been engaged in conflict](75005425.md), as part of [a longer stretch of hostilities between the two](62111540.md) since the latter's founding in 1982. Hezbollah launched strikes against Israel after the 7 October 2023 attacks, which it said was in solidarity with the Palestinians.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] After a year of fighting, Israel [invaded southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon) in October 2024, pulling out after the US brokered an [agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement) between Israel and Lebanon at the end of November, but not before [killing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike) Hezbollah's militant leader, cleric [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md). Between [November 2024 and March 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hezbollah_conflict_\(27_November_2024_%E2%80%93_present\)), however, several ceasefire violations occurred between the two, with Israel launching near-daily attacks[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] killing 331 people (of which at least 127 were civilians) by November 2025;[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-77] later, the total death toll reached 500;[^cite_note-78] Israel said these actions were an attempt to enforce the ceasefire terms and prevent Hezbollah's renewed military buildup.[^cite_note-79] Hezbollah violated the ceasefire terms by rebuilding its militant infrastructure and weapons arsenal.[^cite_note-hezvio-80]

[UNIFIL](787766.md), a UN peacekeeping mission present in southern Lebanon for decades, was terminated in September 2025 by the UN Security Council (amid pressure from Israel and the US, a permanent member of the council); its mandate ends on 31 December 2026 and it is expected to disappear from Lebanon in 2027 at the latest.[^cite_note-81]

Following the [US–Israeli joint strikes on Iran](82546969.md) on 28 February 2026, Hezbollah condemned the attacks, stating "we are confident that the American and Israeli enemy will receive a great slap and will reap nothing but failure from its tyrannical, criminal aggression."[^cite_note-82] On 1 March, after Iran confirmed [the death](82537558.md) of [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) in the US–Israeli strikes, Hezbollah's secretary-general [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) vowed to retaliate and "undertake our duty of confronting the aggression", stating Hezbollah would not leave "the field of honor and resistance."[^cite_note-83] Hezbollah later claimed that the attack was a "defensive act" after over a year of Israeli [attacks](75005425.md) despite a [truce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement).[^cite_note-Namibia-Press-Agency-2026-84] It added that it restarted fighting to force Israel to stop its aggression and evacuate from seized Lebanese territories, emphasizing that the move was unrelated to the [Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-United-Press-International-2026-85]

## Main events

Main article: [Timeline of the 2026 Lebanon war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_2026_Lebanon_war)

See also: [List of engagements during the 2026 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_engagements_during_the_2026_Lebanon_War)

### First week (2–8 March)

On 2 March, Hezbollah launched several projectiles into northern Israel, the first time it had done so since the [2024 ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement), targeting a [missile defense](1099196.md) site south of [Haifa](55606.md).[^cite_note-Bernama-2026-86] Hezbollah said it restarted fighting to force Israel to stop its aggression and evacuate from [seized Lebanese territories](6020082.md), emphasising that the move was unrelated to the [Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-United-Press-International-2026-85] The [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) said it had intercepted one projectile originating from Lebanon, letting several others fall into open sites.[^cite_note-87] In response, Israeli jets struck targets in the Lebanese capital of Beirut at 3am local time,[^cite_note-88] issuing evacuation orders to local civilians in 50 villages across [Southern Lebanon](4205968.md) and the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md).[^cite_note-ALJ-89] The Israeli military said it carried out "precise and targeted" strikes against "senior terrorist elements of the Hezbollah terrorist organization in the Beirut area" and "a central terrorist element of the Hezbollah terrorist organization in southern Lebanon."[^cite_note-L'Orient-90] Early reports suggested the intended targets were [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) and [Mohammad Raad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Raad).[^cite_note-Lehmann-91] Later, Saudi news channel [Al Hadath](6315937.md) reported Raad's whereabouts were unknown and stated his body was being searched for in the rubble.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] The IDF said Hezbollah's intelligence chief, Hussain Makled, was killed in the strikes.[^cite_note-94] Adham Adnan al-Othman, senior commander of the [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) in Lebanon, was also killed in an airstrike in Beirut's southern suburbs.[^cite_note-lot-jihad-95]

On 3 March, Hezbollah fired missiles and drones targeting three Israeli bases, [Ramat David Airbase](5340731.md), the [Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meron,_Israel) monitoring base and [Camp Yitzhak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Yitzhak).[^cite_note-france24.com-96][^cite_note-97] Israel carried out airstrikes on Hezbollah strongholds and also targeted [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar) TV's headquarters in Beirut.[^cite_note-98] Later on in the day, Israeli forces were ordered to seize positions within Lebanon.[^cite_note-99] Israel said its military had seized "strategic areas" in southern Lebanon while ordering nearby Lebanese towns to evacuate.[^cite_note-100] Israel struck [Yohmor](31574515.md) with [white phosphorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_phosphorus_munition), illegal under international law, causing several homes to catch fire.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-102]

On 4 March, Hezbollah launched drone and missiles into Israel, attempting to hit military bases and oil and gas infrastructures.[^cite_note-english.nepalnews.com-103] Hezbollah anti-tank fire caused moderate injuries to two Israeli soldiers in southern Lebanon, making them the first Israeli injuries of the conflict.[^cite_note-Boxerman-2026-104] Israel launched strikes targeting the communications centres of Hezbollah, during which al-Manar TV station and [al-Nour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nour) radio station were struck.[^cite_note-105] More strikes were focused on villages in southern Lebanon used by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-english.nepalnews.com-103] Following Hezbollah's continued attacks, Israel issued an immediate urgent evacuation warning to South Lebanon residents, telling them to move north of the [Litani River](1920925.md).[^cite_note-106] UN peacekeeping force reported Israeli soldiers entered several towns and villages in Southern Lebanon, including [Kfar Kila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Kila), [Houla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houla), [Kfar Shouba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Shouba), [Yaroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaroun) and [Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam).[^cite_note-107]

Israel carried out an incursion into southern [Syria](7515849.md) on 4 March, shelling the area between [Jamla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamla) and Saisoun in [Daraa Governorate](5317030.md), and arrested four civilians. Israel accused Syria of deploying combat units to the Syrian [Golan Heights](7515865.md), stating it will not allow the Hezbollah–Israel strikes to be used as a pretext to "harm the [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze)" in southern Syria and demanded the Syrian government prevent [Iraqi militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_militias) from crossing Syrian territory towards the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109]

UNIFIL published on 5 March that since 2 March it monitored over 210 missiles fired by Hezbollah into Israel.[^cite_note-110] Israel killed a Hamas official in a strike in Beirut, as well as several strikes early in the day, including two in the Hezbollah stronghold of south Beirut. More attacks continued, targeting Hezbollah facilities and operatives, as it renewed its evacuation warning to residence in Southern Lebanon, close to sites it will target.[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] At midday, the IDF published an immediate evacuation notice to the southern suburbs of Beirut, specifically [Bourj el-Barajneh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bourj_el-Barajneh), [Hadath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadath,_Mount_Lebanon), [Haret Hreik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haret_Hreik) and [Chiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiyah). Residents were advised by the IDF to move north and east.[^cite_note-114]

After Israel ordered inhabitants of the entire portion of Lebanon that is south of the Litani River to evacuate (along with some other areas), Hezbollah issued its own orders to civilians in a five-kilometer-wide strip of Israeli territory bordering Lebanon to evacuate. This message was published by Hezbollah in Hebrew, along with maps of the zone to be evacuated. Israeli sources described Hezbollah's self-described evacuation orders as psychological warfare; Israel said it is not evacuating the area. Hezbollah repeated the supposed evacuation order in the following days and weeks.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117]

Hezbollah conducted a series of attacks on Israel and Israeli forces on 6 March, launching rockets and artillery shells toward the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) and [Haifa](55606.md).[^cite_note-118] During the night and morning after issuing advance warnings to minimize civilian casualties, Israel launched a series of airstrikes targeting Hezbollah headquarters located in ten high-rise buildings in Beirut. The strikes also targeted warehouses where drones used in attacks against Israel were reportedly stored.[^cite_note-119] A Hezbollah projectile fired near the Lebanese border injured eight Israeli soldiers, five severely.[^cite_note-120] Among the injured was the son of far-right finance minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md).[^cite_note-121] Two Israeli missile strikes hit a UNIFIL battalion headquarters in [al-Qaouzah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaouzah) in [Bint Jbeil](6124393.md), injuring four UN peacekeepers from [Ghana](12067.md).[^cite_note-Ghana_peacekeeper_injuries-57][^cite_note-122]

On 7 March, Hezbollah continued firing missiles and rockets into northern Israel; no deaths were reported.[^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124] Israel's retaliation included attacks in the eastern Lebanese town of [Nabi Chit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabi_Chit), were reports say an operation to search for [Ron Arad](8015452.md) took place.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] The Lebanese health ministry reported that Israeli military killed over 41 people there, and injured 40 more.[^cite_note-128] The deaths include at least three soldiers of the [Lebanese Armed Forces](17779.md) and one from Lebanon's [General Security Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Security_Directorate_\(Lebanon\)).[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-20260307-36] Later that day an evacuation notice was issued by the IDF to [Zqouq El Mufdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zqouq_El_Mufdi?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Tyre](30796.md).[^cite_note-129] These evacuation notices were followed by "Save your lives" notices and reminders to residents to stay away from their homes.[^cite_note-130]

Israel launched an airstrike on the Ramada hotel building in central Beirut on 8 March, killing four key Iranian commanders of the IRGC's elite [Quds Force](4028355.md), who were planning terror attacks on Israel.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] Later the Israeli army stated they killed five senior commanders from the Lebanon Corps of the Quds Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps in a precise strike in Beirut.[^cite_note-133] Netanyahu said: "Whoever lays down their arms will save their life, and whoever doesn't, their blood will be spilled."[^cite_note-134] A Hezbollah rocket attack in southern Lebanon damaged a [Puma armored engineering vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puma_armored_engineering_vehicle) and then hit a [Caterpillar D9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_D9) bulldozer attempting to assist the vehicle, killing two soldiers.[^cite_note-The_Jerusalem_Post-20260308-135]

[Constantinos Kombos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantinos_Kombos), [Cyprus](5593.md)'s Foreign Minister, stated the explosive-packed drones launched from Lebanon towards the island, were launched by Hezbollah, targeting [British bases](1025144.md) there.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137]

#### Airstrike on Ghazali family home (5 March)

| Ghazali family killing |  |
| --- | --- |
| [Map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:Map/6/33.528055555556/35.651666666667/en) |  |
| Location | [33°31′41″N 35°39′6″E  /  33.52806°N 35.65167°E  / 33.52806; 35.65167](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=2026_Lebanon_war\&params=33_31_41_N_35_39_6_E_region\:LB-BI_type\:event) [Machghara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machghara) |
| Date | 5 March 2026 at sunset |
| Target | Ibrahim Ghazali |
| Attack type | [Assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_assassinations_in_Lebanon) ([targeted killing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeted_killing_by_Israel)) with other casualties |
| Weapon | Airstrike |
| Deaths | 4 (2 men + 2 children)  Ibrahim Ghazali Ibrahim's children: Ali and Fatima Ibrahim's brother: Kassim Ghazali |
| Injured | 4 (both men's wives and parents) |
| Perpetrator | [Israel](9282173.md) [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) [Israel](9282173.md) [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) |

On the evening of 5 March 2026, four members of the Ghazali family were killed by an Israeli airstrike on their home in [Mashghara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mashghara), southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-NPR_Al-Shalchi-138][^cite_note-Sahouri_Warikoo-139][^cite_note-NBC_12_March_2026-140] The attack on the family home occurred while they were gathered for [Iftar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iftar), the meal that breaks the fast at the end of the day in [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan).[^cite_note-NPR_Al-Shalchi-138][^cite_note-Sahouri_Warikoo-139] The men's maternal uncle, Fouad Qasem, who lived nearby, helped recover the bodies. He told [NPR](29697232.md), "I held my own flesh and blood in my hands... What did the children do to deserve this?"[^cite_note-141]

On 8 March, a memorial service was held for the family in [Dearborn Heights, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn_Heights,_Michigan), where other family members lived. The service was attended by hundreds of people.[^cite_note-Streeter-142][^cite_note-Kershner-143] One of the family members in Michigan was United States citizen Ayman Ghazali, the men's brother and the children's uncle.[^cite_note-Streeter-142][^cite_note-Kershner-143] On 12 March, Ayman Ghazali [attacked the Temple Israel synagogue](82661834.md). Ayman's suicide was the only death in the attack, but a security guard was injured and responding police officers were treated for smoke inhalation.[^cite_note-144]

On 15 March, the IDF claimed responsibility for the attack in Lebanon, alleging that Ibrahim Ghazali (the children's father) was a Hezbollah commander and describing Ibrahim as "eliminated", the term the [IDF uses for assassinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeted_killing_by_Israel).[^cite_note-Makor_Rishon_15_March_2026-145][^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] The IDF's Arabic language spokesperson [Avichai Adraee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avichai_Adraee) further alleged that Ibrahim Ghazali was in Hezbollah's anti-tank missile unit.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] In response to the statements by Israel, a Hezbollah spokesperson said that none of the family members were affiliated with Hezbollah.[^cite_note-150]

### Second week (9–15 March)

Further information: [Killing of Pierre al-Rahi](82631603.md)

A double strike on a house in [Al-Qlayaa](51590325.md) killed Lebanese [Maronite Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maronite_Catholic) priest Father [Pierre al-Rahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_al-Rahi).[^cite_note-151] According to Hezbollah, on 9 March the organization fought Israeli forces that landed once again in eastern Lebanon near Nabi Sheet. This time they were 15 Israeli helicopters that came from the Syrian side of the border.[^cite_note-152] Hezbollah kept firing rocket and missiles in to Israel.[^cite_note-153] During the day Israel targeted strikes on the [Al-Qard Al-Hasan Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qard_Al-Hasan_Association), an US-sanctioned institution known for funding Hezbollah.[^cite_note-154] Before mid night, the [Syrian Army](3423927.md) informed Hezbollah shelled their positions.[^cite_note-155] The Syrian Army declared they detected Hezbollah's reinforcements at the Syrian-Lebanese border.[^cite_note-156]

The Syrian Army said they would coordinate a possible Syrian response to Hezbollah's actions with the Lebanese government side.[^cite_note-157] On the same day, Syria reported early in the morning that artillery shells fired from Lebanon landed near a town 20 miles west of [Damascus](8914.md). Syria accused Hezbollah of targeting Syrian army positions in the area and said that it had observed Hezbollah reinforcements arriving along the [Lebanon–Syria border](46232625.md), warning that it would respond to any attack on its territory.[^cite_note-Iwaza-2026-17]

Hezbollah claimed it targeted sites in Tell al-Hamames, south of the city of Khiam.[^cite_note-158] According to reports Hezbollah and Iran fired missiles on northern Israel.[^cite_note-159] It was reported that residents from Aalma El Chaeb in South Lebanon, left the town towards Tyre, escorted by LAF and UNIFIL forces.[^cite_note-160] Later Israel issued warning to residence of Arnoun, Yohmor, Zawtar El Charqiyeh, and Zawtar El Gharbiyeh residents to evacuate.[^cite_note-161]



Israeli soldier in Southern Lebanon, 11 March

Lebanon's state-run National News Agency, reported about airstrikes over night on 11 March, that were later confirmed by the Israeli military.[^cite_note-162] It was followed by an evacuation notice issued to six villages in south Lebanon.[^cite_note-163] The IDF confirmed Iranian commander Ali Musallam Tabaja was killed during the airstrikes.[^cite_note-164] On 12 March, an Israeli airstrike on the outskirts of the village of [Ain Ebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_Ebel) killed three men.[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167]

Lebanon's health ministry said on 13 March that an Israeli airstrike hit a health center in [Burj Qalaouiyah](51773672.md) killing 12 people—nearly the entire medical team of paramedics, doctors, and nurses—leaving only one severely injured worker alive. Four others remain missing.[^cite_note-168]

During the night of 13/14 March, [fighting continued in Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Khiam_\(2026\)).[^cite_note-\:0-169][^cite_note-170] Israel continued to conduct airstrikes across Lebanon on 14 March, including areas outside of Hezbollah's operations. An airstrike on an apartment in [Bourj Hammoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bourj_Hammoud) in northeastern Beirut killed at least one person and wounded four others.[^cite_note-171]

### Third week (16–22 March)



Aftermath of an airstrike in [Bachoura, Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachoura,_Beirut), on 18 March



IDF [162nd Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/162nd_Division_\(Israel\)) on the [Israel–Lebanon border](987817.md), 21 March

The IDF announced on 16 March that it had begun a "targeted ground operation against key targets" in southern Lebanon, aimed at establishing "the forward defense area." Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) said that such operations would continue until Hezbollah no longer posed a threat to northern Israeli communities, and ruled out displaced Lebanese people returning to their homes in the south until then. He added that the operations will resemble the [Israeli invasion of the Gaza Strip](74998519.md), "just as was done against [Hamas](13913.md) in [Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Beit_Hanoun) and the terror tunnels in Gaza." Hezbollah Secretary-General [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) has been designated a target for elimination.[^cite_note-The_Times_of_Israel-20260316-172]

Four injuries were reported in northern Israel as a result of Hezbollah rocket attacks on the area, including three in [Nahariya](323332.md) and one in [Kibbutz Kabri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabri,_Israel).[^cite_note-173] An Israeli soldier from the Givati Brigade was injured during an operational accident.[^cite_note-174] An Israeli airstrike hit a building near Jabal Amel Hospital outside [Tyre](30796.md) killed one person and injured five others.[^cite_note-175] Fighting was reported at the Hezbollah stronghold of [Al-Khiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khiyam) in southern Lebanon where a "major battle was under way" with at least three airstrikes in the city.[^cite_note-\:0-169] Israel also struck targets in [Yatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yatar), [Burj Qalaouiyah](51773672.md), [as-Sultaniyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/As-Sultaniyah), [Shaqra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaqra,_Lebanon), [Qantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qantara,_Marjayoun), and as-Sawana.[^cite_note-176]

[Mahmoud Qamati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud_Qamati), Hezbollah's political council vice president, threatened to topple the [cabinet of Nawaf Salam](78886120.md) after the war ends, comparing it to [Vichy France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vichy_France)'s regime.[^cite_note-177] The [Kuwaiti Ministry of Interior](40538189.md) said it arrested a 16-member Hezbollah cell with weapons, drone and encrypted communications devices, which planned an attack.[^cite_note-178] On 17 March, the IDF said it had deployed a second division to southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-179] launching a ground invasion. Israel Katz said the operation would be similar to the [Gaza war](74998519.md), suggested that Israel could occupy some Lebanese territory indefinitely and ruled out the return of displaced Lebanese people to their homes as long as Hezbollah remained a threat.[^cite_note-180] In a joint statement, Canada, France, Germany, Italy, and the UK said such an operation should be avoided.[^cite_note-181] Syria was pressured to militarily intervene in Lebanon by the United States by sending its troops and striking positions in Lebanon alongside the IDF in order to further weaken Hezbollah;[^cite_note-182] however, the Syrian government refused the offer on the same day Israel Katz declared a ground invasion of southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-183] [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), [Qatar](8238258.md), [Egypt](8087628.md), and [Turkey](11125639.md) discouraged a confrontation between Syria and Hezbollah.[^cite_note-184]

Israel conducted extensive overnight strikes in central Beirut on 18 March, which were largely unannounced with no warnings issued, killing at least 10 people and injuring 27.[^cite_note-185] Two Israeli soldiers were wounded by a Hezbollah mortar attack in northern Israel on 21 March.[^cite_note-186] Civilians were also injured as munitions fired by Hezbollah struck civilian buildings in northern Israeli border towns, including Ma'alot.[^cite_note-187]





The Qasmiyeh Bridge after it was destroyed by Israeli forces

Israel's Defense Minister Katz declared that the IDF would demolish the Lebanese villages bordering Israel and destroy all the Litani river crossings.[^cite_note-188] Following a warning, Israel destroyed the Qasmiyeh Bridge, the main bridge over the Litani river, connecting Lebanon and south Lebanon.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] Lebanese prime minister Nawaf Salam said that Hezbollah's operations are commanded by IRGC officers.[^cite_note-191] The IDF reported that seven soldiers were wounded on Lebanon in separate incidents.[^cite_note-TTOI1-192] IDF Chief of General Staff [Eyal Zamir](61877756.md) approved plans "to advance the targeted ground operations and strikes" against Hezbollah, and that this would be a "prolonged operation".[^cite_note-193] The Israeli military said it killed Abu Khalil Barji, a commander in Hezbollah's [Radwan Force](69191358.md), and two other Hezbollah operatives in an air strike in the Majdal Selem area of southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-194] That same day, an Israeli avocado farmer was killed from a misfired artillery by the IDF near the Israeli–Lebanese border.[^cite_note-195] Hezbollah official [Wafiq Safa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wafiq_Safa) in an interview admitted Hezbollah had started the war for revenge over the [assassination of Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Khamenei) and threatened the Lebanese government with the repeat of [the 7 May clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Lebanon_conflict).[^cite_note-196]

### Fourth week (23–29 March)

On 23 March the IDF struck the Dallafa Bridge in southern Lebanon, which it accuses Hezbollah of using to send militants and weapons to fight Israel.[^cite_note-197] Finance Minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) stated that Israel, following the war, should annex territories up to the [Litani River](1920925.md).[^cite_note-198] Satellite imagery from 23 March showed the remains of the [Khiam detention center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam_detention_center) being demolished.[^cite_note-199]

The Lebanese government declared the Iranian ambassador [Mohammad Reza Sheibani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Sheibani) [persona non grata](521241.md) on 24 March, giving him time until Sunday to leave.[^cite_note-200] According to Iran International (which opposes the current Iranian regime) Iran fired a ballistic missile at Lebanon several hours after the expulsion of the ambassador was announced, and the projectile struck Lebanon's coast in Sahel Alma (Keserwan District).[^cite_note-201] Other reports indicate that Sahel Alma was only hit by debris, and that it occurred when an Israeli interceptor missile shot down, over [Keserwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keserwan_District), a ballistic missile launched from Iran.[^cite_note-202] Various sources from the US military intel heavily suggest the target was located on Cyprus.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206]

Israel's defence minister, Israel Katz openly stated that the military will take control of south Lebanon all the way to the Litani river,[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] mirroring the claims of Smotrich from the previous day. [Hassan Fadlallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Fadlallah), a lawmaker of Hezbollah, vowed that the group would fight Israel to prevent the plan from coming to fruition, labelling it as one of the goals of the organization during the war.[^cite_note-209]

Also on 24 March, an Israeli drone strike targeted the residence of a teacher in [Ain Ebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ain_Ebel), but the family members on the ground floor survived unharmed.[^cite_note-210] Israel reported that missile fire from Lebanon killed one Israeli civilian and wounded another two.[^cite_note-211] Israeli Defense minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) states that the military will control a "[security zone](83293911.md)" up to the [Litani River](1920925.md) in southern Lebanon until the threat of [Hezbollah](13919.md) is removed.[^cite_note-212]

Nethanyahu followed Katz's and Smotrich's statement on 25 March calling for the creation of a "security/buffer zone" in Southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-213] Iran asserted that Lebanon must be included as part of a ceasefire deal with the United States and Israel, thereby conditioning a ceasefire in the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) on a cessation of hostilities against Hezbollah.[^cite_note-214] Two IDF soldiers were killed by Hezbollah attacks on Southern Lebanon on 26 March.[^cite_note-R26-215] One Israeli civilian was killed by Hezbollah rocket fire in Nahariyah.[^cite_note-216] The IDF said that it was sending more troops from the [162nd Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/162nd_Division_\(Israel\)) to join two other army divisions in southern Lebanon to expand the "buffer zone" in the area.[^cite_note-217]

The IDF made relatively rapid advances into southern Lebanon on 27 March, advancing north past the coastal town of [Naqoura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naqoura) toward Bayada. Hezbollah said that it had targeted an Israeli tank in Bayada.[^cite_note-218] An Israeli airstrike in southern Lebanon on 28 March killed two prominent Lebanese television journalists: Ali Choeib (also known as Ali Shaib) who worked for the Hezbollah-affiliated [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar) and Fatima Ftouni, who worked for the pro-Hezbollah [Al Mayadeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mayadeen). The IDF stated that Choeib had been exposing Israeli military positions, and described him as a member of [Radwan force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radwan_force), Hezbollah's special operations unit.[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] Israeli military posted a photo of Choeib in military fatigues on its [X account](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_account) and when asked for a source of the image, a military spokesperson said "it was photoshopped".[^cite_note-222]

An IDF soldier was reported killed by a Hezbollah attack, while another three soldiers were wounded in south Lebanon.[^cite_note-TOI29-223] Israel killed two Lebanese paramedics in [Bint Jbeil](6124393.md) with airstrikes, and later accused Hezbollah of making "extensive military use" of ambulances and other medical facilities.[^cite_note-224] Lebanese military sources told [Al Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English) that Israeli troops had bypassed [Aitaroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aitaroun) and reached the outskirts of [Wadi al-Salouqi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadi_al-Salouqi?action=edit\&redlink=1), at the same time entering the town of [Bayada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayada,_Lebanon), advanced towards the Litani from below the town of [Qantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qantara,_Marjayoun) near [al-Muhaysibat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Muhaysibat?action=edit\&redlink=1) and also east of it.[^cite_note-225]

The Iranian diplomatic source told [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) the Iranian ambassador who is declared *[persona non grata](521241.md)* defied the Lebanese government's order to leave.[^cite_note-226]

Syrian authorities launched a crackdown on tunnels along the border with Lebanon used for smuggling arms by Hezbollah. The Syrian defense ministry announced the discovery and closure of two tunnels on the border of Lebanon used for smuggling by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-syria30march-18]

### Fifth week (30 March – 5 April)

Further information: [2026 attack on UNIFIL post in Aadchit al-Qusayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_attack_on_UNIFIL_post_in_Aadchit_al-Qusayr)

On 29 March, a UNIFIL position was shelled by Israel, killing a UNIFIL peacekeeper from Indonesia.[^cite_note-227] On 30 March, [two more UNIFIL peacekeepers from Indonesia were killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_attack_on_UNIFIL_post_in_Aadchit_al-Qusayr),[^cite_note-228] this time when one vehicle in a convoy was hit by an explosion. In this case, the UN states they were most likely killed by a tripwire-activated explosive device planted by Hezbollah, because another explosive device of this kind was found nearby.[^cite_note-229]

Also on 30 March, a UNIFIL patrol was stopped by Hezbollah in [Deir Qanoun an-Naher](51731210.md) on its main street and ordered to leave the area, which the patrol refused, resulting in gunfire from both sides.[^cite_note-DeirQanoun-15]

Israeli media reported the death of four IDF soldiers in clashes with Hezbollah, three of whom were the from the Nahal Brigade's Reconnaissance Unit.[^cite_note-230]

On 1 April, an [Israeli Navy](1370788.md) strike in Beirut killed the commander of Hezbollah's Southern Front, Hajj Yusuf Ismail Hashem, whose predecessor [Ali Karaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Karaki) was killed in September 2024. Hezbollah confirmed Hashem's death, stating that it was their harshest loss since the killing of [Haytham Ali Tabatabai](81670215.md) in November 2025.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232] An IDF soldier was killed and another wounded on 4 April after a friendly fire incident in Sheeba southern Lebanon, during a commando raid.[^cite_note-Fabian-2026-31]

The IDF hit a four-story building on 4 April, part of a church-sponsored social housing complex, in Ain Saade, a Christian locality not far from Beirut, killing at least three people including local politician Pierre Moawad (of [Lebanese Forces](2113218.md), a party which currently forms the ruling coalition in Lebanon and is opposed to Hezbollah), his wife, and their female neighbor.[^cite_note-lorient1-233][^cite_note-lorient2-234] The IDF said they were targeting a Hezbollah member hiding among local Christians,[^cite_note-lorient1-233][^cite_note-lorient2-234] and that Moawad was not the intended target.[^cite_note-\:reuterslf-235] Misinformation about the strike circulated online included a faked memo (a voice note) supposedly left by Pierre Moawad before his death. An account about a man who left the building and drove away on a motorcycle roughly around the time of the strike was relayed by the slain couple's daughter to journalists; media reports quoting the Lebanese army said the man on the motorcycle was a delivery driver, who had been delivering medications to one of the building's residents for a few months[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] but *L'Orient Today* later wrote that, per an anonymous judicial source, the man was an unnamed Hezbollah official visiting his girlfriend. There has been speculation that Israeli strikes on Christian communities in Lebanon are aimed at inflaming sectarian tensions.[^cite_note-\:reuterslf-235]

### Sixth week (6–12 April)



Smoke rises after an Israeli strike on Beirut during the 8 April attacks.

On 6 April, IDF spokesman Avichay Adraee ordered the southern suburbs of Beirut to be evacuated, because Israel will strike "Hezbollah infrastructure" there.[^cite_note-238] The [Israeli Ground Forces](64121347.md) deployed the [98th Division](12871166.md) into southern Lebanon on 7 April, in addition to the [36th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/36th_Division_\(IDF\)), [91st](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/91st_Division_\(IDF\)), [146th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/146th_Division_\(Israel\)), and [162nd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/162nd_Division_\(IDF\)) divisions already there. The stated goal of their operation was to destroy buildings near the border being used for military purposes and to push Hezbollah members north of the Litani River. According to the Israeli paper *[Yedioth Ahronoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yedioth_Ahronoth)*, the IDF was preparing a plan to continue the operations and ground occupation in Lebanon even after the Iran war ends.[^cite_note-AA-07042026-239] Hezbollah said on the same day that ["fierce clashes"](82919076.md) were taking place between its fighters and Israeli troops in Bint Jbeil, and that rocket and drone strikes targeted soldiers and [Merkava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkava) tanks in the town, and in other settlements in southern Lebanon or northern Israel. Israeli troops destroyed buildings in [Khiam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khiam) and other border towns.[^cite_note-240]

Israel agreed to a two-week ceasefire with Iran as part of the [7 April 2026 Iran war ceasefire](82888027.md).[^cite_note-241] Although Pakistani prime minister [Shehbaz Sharif](6502561.md) said that the ceasefire includes all fronts of the war, including Lebanon,[^cite_note-242] Israel rejected Sharif's condition, asserting that the ceasefire "does not include Lebanon."[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] The United States has also said that the ceasefire does not include Lebanon.[^cite_note-245] Hezbollah said that it had halted attacks on Israel and on Israeli soldiers in Lebanon.[^cite_note-246]

Hours after the ceasefire announcement, Israel launched [Operation Eternal Darkness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Eternal_Darkness) on 8 April, which, per Israel, included a wide-ranging operation targeting all of Hezbollah's command and control centers in southern Lebanon, Beirut and the Bekaa Valley.[^cite_note-247] The attacks were the largest since the start of the war,[^cite_note-Reuters-08042026-248] with Israel saying that its military hit over 100 targets within ten minutes.[^cite_note-Reuters-08042026-248][^cite_note-Yahoo-08042026-249] The attacks have killed at least 357 people.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] One IDF soldier was killed and five wounded in clashes with Hezbollah fighters in South Lebanon.[^cite_note-ToIC1-253]

In the early hours of 9 April, Hezbollah said it launched a missile attack against northern Israel.[^cite_note-254] Meanwhile, in Lebanon, Israeli strikes continued.[^cite_note-255] Later, the IDF announced that it had killed Hezbollah leader Naim Qassem's personal secretary and nephew, [Ali Yusuf Harshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Yusuf_Harshi?action=edit\&redlink=1) in a strike on Beirut.[^cite_note-256] Hezbollah was also reported to have fired "dozens" of rockets into Israel from Lebanon.[^cite_note-257] At around 17\:00 [Israel Daylight Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Daylight_Time), *[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post)* reported that the 98th Division of the IDF had almost fully taken control of all of the town of Bint Jbeil,[^cite_note-258] which had become the main site of ground combat between Hezbollah and the IDF.[^cite_note-RTE-10042026-259]

On early 10 April, Israel launched a wave of strikes across Lebanon, killing at least 28 people, including 13 [Lebanese State Security](30443400.md) personnel at an office in [Nabatieh](43794798.md).[^cite_note-AJ-10042026-40][^cite_note-260] During a visit to Israeli commanders near Bint Jbeil, IDF chief of staff Eyal Zamir said that the IDF is in a "state of war" in Lebanon, despite the ceasefire with Iran.[^cite_note-261]

On 12 April, a Lebanese security source informed that the Israeli army cut off all main roads leading to besieged Bint Jbeil and kept pounding it with air strikes, artillery shells and white phosphorus bombs,[^cite_note-262] but still hasn't been yet able to capture important sites within the city itself,[^cite_note-263] with clashes occurring all over the city outskirts.[^cite_note-264] Prime Minister Netanyahu and Defense Minister Katz visited Israeli troops inside Lebanon, with Katz saying that Israel will eliminate the threat "just as we did in Gaza," including with the demolition of homes so that they cannot be used as "terror outposts."[^cite_note-NPR-14042026-265]

### Seventh week (13–19 April)

On 13 April, the IDF said to have killed over 100 Hezbollah fighters in Bint Jbeil in the past several days[^cite_note-266] and to have successfully encircled the city,[^cite_note-267] stating the complete takeover is expected to take a few more days.[^cite_note-268] One IDF sergeant was killed in combat in Southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-JPC1-269]

On 15 April, Israel and Hezbollah forces in Lebanon engaged in more strikes against each other.[^cite_note-270] A day later, two Israeli airstrikes destroyed the Qasmiyeh Bridge, the last remaining link between the area south of the Litani River and the rest of the country.[^cite_note-271]

On 16 April, President Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon agreed to a [10-day truce](82970933.md), after his conversations with Prime Minister Netanyahu and President Aoun.[^cite_note-272] The ceasefire entered into force on the night of 16/17 April local time. The IDF said the death of Ali Reda Abbas and other unnamed Hezbollah commanders, and alleged to have eliminated over 150 militants by the end of operations.[^cite_note-273] Israeli defense minister Katz said that the IDF will hold its positions in southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-274] and the Israeli military warned residents of the area to remain north of the Litani River, while the Lebanese military reported several violations of the ceasefire by Israel.[^cite_note-275] The violations reportedly took place with attacks in Khiam and Bint Jbeil, and shelling in [Dibbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dibbin). According to IDF commanders, residents will not be allowed to return to 55 villages that are in its operational zone, and PM Netanyahu stressed that the fight against Hezbollah is not over.[^cite_note-276]

On 17 April, Israel killed one Hezbollah member in a drone strike on a motorcycle, and throughout 17 April and 18 April killed other Hezbollah members in several other strikes in Lebanon, in at least two cases stating that the Hezbollah members posed a threat. On 17 April, an IDF soldier was killed in Lebanon after entering a building previously rigged with explosives by Hezbollah. On 18 April, another IDF soldier was killed and 9 others wounded, one severely, when one of IDF bulldozers drove over an explosive device. Israel continued to perform demolitions of buildings in Israeli-occupied areas of Lebanon.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279]

Israel said it continued strikes on military targets, which came in violation of the ceasefire. According to a security source familiar to *[Asharq Al-Awsat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asharq_Al-Awsat)*, the violations included artillery bombardments, drone strikes, house-to-house fighting, laying [booby traps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booby_traps) and destruction of houses.[^cite_note-280]

A French soldier was killed by gunfire and three others injured in an attack on UNIFIL peacekeepers in [Deir Kifa](68829886.md) on 18 April. French president Emmanuel Macron attributed the attack to Hezbollah, which the group denied.[^cite_note-AP-18042026-51] Another French UNIFIL peacekeeper who was wounded in the attack died on 22 April from his injuries.[^cite_note-Ghandourieh-52]

The Israeli military announced on the same day that it established a "yellow line" in southern Lebanon, like it did in Gaza.[^cite_note-281] The zone which Israel intends to control, as marked on a map published by the IDF, covers a larger area than Israeli ground forces controlled at the moment of the ceasefire: a wider belt of Lebanon's southernmost areas, plus new areas of the [Marjayoun District](8968039.md) and even some areas north of the Litani River; and an area around the town of [Shebaa](42907053.md) (not to be confused with [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms)) and northeast of it.[^cite_note-282] In total, the zone encompasses 55 towns and villages.[^cite_note-283] Israeli ground forces continued military operations within that zone during the ceasefire.[^cite_note-284]

On the afternoon of 19 April, according to Hezbollah, their members used explosive devices against a column of Israeli tanks (between [Taybeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taybeh,_Marjayoun) and [Deir Siryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Siryan)).[^cite_note-285]

Main article: [Desecration of Christian statues in Debel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desecration_of_Christian_statues_in_Debel)

Widespread outrage has erupted following the circulation of a viral photograph and video showing an Israeli soldier smashing a statue of [Jesus Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_Christ) with a [sledgehammer](910251.md) in the southern Lebanese village of Debel. The incident has drawn condemnation from religious leaders, international figures, and the Israeli government. The incident occurred in Debel on 19 April 2026, shortly after a ceasefire began. Videos and photos show a soldier in uniform striking a fallen statue of Christ at a small residential shrine.[^cite_note-286]

### Eighth week (20–26 April)

Israel warned residents of southern Lebanon to stay out of the area,[^cite_note-287] and as of 21 April, Israeli troops were still battling Hezbollah forces in spite of the ceasefire.[^cite_note-288] On the same day, Israeli forces said that Hezbollah violated the ceasefire by launching rockets at an Israeli position in [Rab Thalathin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rab_Thalathin) and a drone into northern Israel. It added that it hit the rocket launcher as a response to one of those incidents. Hezbollah said that it fired rockets and attack drones towards a site in northern Israel that it said was the source of artillery shelling towards a town in south Lebanon in retaliation for what it said were Israeli violations of the truce.[^cite_note-289]

As of 22 April, Israel continued demolitions of civilian infrastructure in occupied areas of Lebanon; large-scale demolition operations were reported in Khiam "in a scene that suggests an attempt to completely erase the town's identity"[^cite_note-Demolition-290][^cite_note-Western_Bekaa-291] and in Qantara[^cite_note-Western_Bekaa-291] (both in the Marjayoun District). In the Bint Jbeil District, tank/artillery shelling and controlled demolitions of infrastructure continued in and around Rshaf and Mais al-Jabal,[^cite_note-Western_Bekaa-291] and Israel was systematically leveling residential areas of the town of Bint Jbeil itself.[^cite_note-Demolition-290]

Among the places where Israeli strikes were reported on 22 April was Yohmor al-Shaqif, which is north of the Litani River. Hezbollah claimed at least one attack on Israeli soldiers in Qantara and also claimed to have hit an artillery location set up by the IDF in Bayada, south Lebanon, with an explosive-laden drone.[^cite_note-Western_Bekaa-291]

The IDF disclosed that a retired colonel, experienced in drone warfare and currently in active reserve service, was killed in Israel in an off-duty accident unrelated to the war.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293]

An Israeli strike hit a car in [Tiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/At-Tiri); journalists driving behind the car that was hit took shelter in a building, which was also targeted, killing journalist [Amal Khalil](83017126.md).[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295]

Israeli strikes on 23 April included a drone strike on a car near Choukine.[^cite_note-choukine23april-296] Demolition of still-remaining houses was in progress in Deir Siryan.[^cite_note-choukine23april-296][^cite_note-deirsiryan23april-297]

During the night of 23–24 April, the IDF struck Touline (first at 10\:30 pm, then during early morning) and Majdel Zoun, using warplanes and artillery. Both are located on the border (the furthest extent) of the "forward defense zone" that Israel claims in Lebanon. The locality of Khirbet Selm was also attacked. Hezbollah fired a salvo of rockets at the region of Shtula (Israel) early the same night, shortly after 11\:00 p.m.[^cite_note-choukine23april-296][^cite_note-deirsiryan23april-297]

During the same night (afternoon in the United States), US president Donald Trump announced the extension of the ceasefire in Lebanon by three more weeks. Just like a week before,[^cite_note-298] the ceasefire announcement came after a meeting between the Lebanese ambassador and the Israeli ambassador in Washington, D.C.;[^cite_note-choukine23april-296] these meetings are a departure from the rule not to maintain contacts with Israel, which before the current Israeli military operation (March 2026–onwards) was followed by all major political forces in Lebanon.



Hezbollah fighters on a rooftop in Bint Jbeil, 24 April 2026

On 24 April and 25 April, warfare continued, including Israeli strikes in Lebanon, demolitions of houses, and Hezbollah strikes on the IDF in Lebanon.[^cite_note-deirsiryan23april-297][^cite_note-299]

On the evening of 25 April, Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu announced an order to strike "with force" against Hezbollah in Lebanon. On 26 April, eastern parts of the Nabatieh District (which is north of the Litani River)—including areas claimed as part of the "forward defense zone" but not currently controlled by Israeli ground forces—came under intense Israeli fire. Other areas in south Lebanon also witnessed Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-300] Throughout the day, Israeli strikes resulted in 14 fatalities.[^cite_note-301] It was announced that an IDF soldier was killed near Taybeh, Lebanon, by an explosive-laden drone launched by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-302]

### Ninth week (27 April – 3 May)

Israel launched airstrikes on new areas of the Beqaa Valley in eastern Lebanon on 27 April.[^cite_note-303] Also on that day, Hezbollah secretary-general Naim Qassem said that he "categorically rejects" the peace talks between the government of Lebanon and Israel, and that "the resistance" against Israel will continue. Lebanese president Joseph Aoun responded by saying that Lebanon will not sign a "humiliating agreement".[^cite_note-304]

On 28 April, an Israeli Ministry of Defense contractor was killed by a Hezbollah drone while performing demolitions in occupied areas of Lebanon.[^cite_note-IDFcontractor-32] Meanwhile, in [Majdal Zoun](78216314.md), an Israeli strike killed three civil defense members working on the site of a previous strike; two other people were killed at the same site.[^cite_note-liveblog29apr-41] On 29 April, a Lebanese soldier traveling home on a motorcycle was killed by one of the Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-liveblog29apr-41]

On 30 April, an IDF soldier was killed in Qantara by an explosive-laden Hezbollah drone. Elsewhere, an Israeli airstrike killed a Lebanese soldier in his home, along with several members of his family.[^cite_note-IDFCas30052026-305]

Israel expanded its evacuation orders (also referred to as forced displacement orders) to 15 more towns and villages, this time including various towns and villages that surround the larger town of [Nabatieh](43794798.md).[^cite_note-IDFCas30052026-305]

Over two days (30 April and 1 May), fourteen Israeli soldiers, but no civilians, were injured in two separate cases of a Hezbollah drone crossing into Israel and directly striking a vehicle next to which the soldiers were operating. In one case, the destroyed vehicle was an IDF artillery shell transporter.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307]

Daily death tolls from Israeli strikes increased; over 40 people were killed on 1 May 2026, and again over 40 people on 2 May 2026, then 20 people on 3 May 2026 (according to tallies made on the afternoon of each day, which cover the preceding 24-hour period).[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310]

### Tenth week (4–10 May)

Seventeen people were killed in total by Israeli strikes on 4 May 2026. Hezbollah claimed multiple attacks on IDF personnel and equipment in Lebanon. Fighting was reported in an area called Khallet Raj, near the Litani River: the IDF apparently tried to advance from Deir Siryan (southern bank of the river) towards Zawtar Sharqieh (northern bank of the river), and came under Hezbollah fire.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-4may-312]

Six people were killed by Israeli strikes on 5 May 2026.[^cite_note-4may-312][^cite_note-5may-313] In an escalation[^cite_note-314] on 6 May 2026, there were 13 known fatalities[^cite_note-ministry6may-315][^cite_note-316] as Israeli strikes targeted, among other places, Zellaya (West Bekaa) and Saksakieh (Sidon District).[^cite_note-317] In the evening, Ahmed Ghaleb Balout, a senior military commander in Hezbollah's Radwan Force, was reportedly assassinated in an Israeli strike in Beirut,[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] as well as two other commanders in other Israeli attacks.[^cite_note-320]

There were 12 fatalities from Israeli strikes on 7 May 2026.[^cite_note-7may-321][^cite_note-ministry6may-315] An Israeli strike hit a rescue crew from the Islamic Health Committee (a health service which is affiliated with Hezbollah and which operates civil defense and ambulance services in Shia-majority areas) arriving at the site of a previous Israeli strike, killing one crew member. In two other cases their ambulances were fired on by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-7may-321]

There were 32 fatalities from Israeli strikes on 8 May 2026. An attempt by IDF ground forces to advance northward from Bayada was reportedly thwarted by Hezbollah the previous night. Throughout the day Hezbollah launched multiple attacks on IDF soldiers in Lebanon, and also wounded two IDF soldiers on the Israeli side of the border. Hezbollah also fired rockets at Israeli territory after 2 p.m. (no resulting injuries reported in Israel).[^cite_note-8may-322][^cite_note-323] On 9 May, 36 people were killed by Israeli strikes,[^cite_note-ministrymay9-324][^cite_note-ministrymay8-325] and on 10 May the daily death toll was 51, according to tallies made on the afternoon of each day and covering the preceding 24-hour period.[^cite_note-ministrymay9-324][^cite_note-ministrymay10-326][^cite_note-10may-327] On 9 May, an Israeli strike at [Saksakieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saksakiyeh) killed at least seven people, including a child, and injured around 15 others, according to the Lebanese Health Ministry.[^cite_note-328]

On 9 May the IDF reported moderately and/or severely injured soldiers, just like during the previous days, but no deaths. An IDF soldier was killed on 10 May.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

The US announced that Israel–Lebanon talks in Washington, D.C. are scheduled for 14 and 15 May. Lebanon will be represented in these talks by a delegation headed by former ambassador to the US Simon Karam.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332]

### Eleventh week (11–17 May)

There were 23 fatalities from Israeli strikes on 11 May.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-ministrymay10-326]

The IDF made an announcement referring to the same area—the banks of the Litani, between Deir Siryan and Zawtar Sharqieh—where fighting between Hezbollah and the IDF was reported during the previous week; they said the IDF carried out a weeklong [raid during which they crossed the Litani River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Israeli_crossing_of_the_Litani_River), that during the raid they managed to move armored vehicles across the river, and that they carried out engineering activity to allow for easier crossing of the river during future raids, "if required".[^cite_note-334]

The Israeli media reported that on 12 May Hezbollah carried out its largest coordinated drone attack since the beginning of the war. According to the media, the first wave hit Israeli soldiers in Lebanon and, at the same time, a target in northern Israel where Israeli soldiers were stationed; and the second wave, consisting of three drones, hit the same target in northern Israel again. An Israeli security source stated that, from the Israeli perspective, the worrying aspect of this attack was the simultaneous use of multiple drones against the same target. The Israeli army radio commented that in recent weeks, Hezbollah's fiber-optic drones have been able to evade interception by Israeli drone-jamming technology, causing "high casualties" (a few fatalities and a significant number of injured) among IDF troops.[^cite_note-335]

Israeli attacks continued on 12 May and 13 May, including a strike on a civil defense center in Nabatieh, which killed two volunteer rescuers working there.

On the afternoon of 13 May, the known death toll over the past two days was 27 people:[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] 13 were killed on 12 May,[^cite_note-bbc13may-338] and 14 were killed on 13 May.[^cite_note-339] However, by the end of the day it became known that on 13 May alone there were 22 fatalities, 8 of them children.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-bbc13may-338] Three children were killed in a strike in [Arabsalim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabsalim); elsewhere, Israel struck at least three vehicles on a busy highway leading south from Beirut, killing 8 including 2 children, with photographs of burning and burned-out SUVs and cars circulated by international media.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342]

On 14 May, in the first known case of a Hezbollah strike wounding Israeli civilians in weeks, a drone hit a parking lot next to the border, injuring 4 people, 1 critically; according to Israeli reports, the injured were solely civilian workers, who came to an otherwise closed former tourist destination next to the border to perform renovation work there; Hezbollah's statements indicate they believed they were targeting IDF soldiers.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] An Israeli soldier was killed by mortar fire in Lebanon, with the IDF stating the death occurred on 14 May, and another soldier was killed on 15 May.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346]

Over four days, from the afternoon of 13 May until the afternoon of 17 May, the daily death tolls from Israeli strikes averaged 23 people per day.[^cite_note-ministry19may-347][^cite_note-ministry13may-348] On the night of 17 May, a Palestinian Islamic Jihad commander in Lebanon was killed in an Israeli airstrike in Baalbek. His 17-year-old daughter was also killed.[^cite_note-349]

### Twelfth week (18–24 May)

Lebanon's health ministry reported on 18 May that the total death toll surpassed 3,000 people.[^cite_note-350] On 19 May, one particular Israeli strike, in Deir Qanoun al-Nahr, killed 6 men, 3 women and 3 children.[^cite_note-351]

Senior security officials said that this week marks a change in the war tactics into a complex and multi-layered attrition operation. It comes to cause casualties to the IDF, deteriorate the Israeli sense of security, and undermine the operational prerogative along the border.[^cite_note-352]

An IDF major was killed on 19 May when one or more Hezbollah militants, hidden in a disused church, opened fire on Israeli soldiers; this occurred in the ruins of Qaouzah, a border village mostly destroyed by Israeli bombardment in 2024 and currently evacuated.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354]

On 18 May, one of Hezbollah's announcements said they launched a drone attack on a vehicle belonging to an IDF brigade commander (head of the 300th "Bar'am" Brigade).[^cite_note-18may-355] On 20 May, an IDF brigade commander (head of the 401st Armored Brigade), holding a rank of colonel, was severely wounded by an explosive-laden drone.[^cite_note-356] Another officer, a lieutenant colonel, was also wounded.[^cite_note-toi20may-357]

The IDF also announced that elsewhere in Lebanon five[^cite_note-358] soldiers were moderately and/or severely wounded by drones on 20 May.[^cite_note-toi20may-357] Hezbollah said it launched [multiple attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Haddatha) on an IDF force consisting of tanks and infantry near [Haddatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haddatha).[^cite_note-may20-359]

On 22 May, an IDF soldier was killed and another severely wounded when a Hezbollah drone struck the Biranit military camp, on the Israeli side of the border.[^cite_note-04222026TTOI-360] On 24 May, an IDF soldier was killed and another severely wounded in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-361]

### Thirteenth week (25–31 May)

Main article: [Battle of Zawtar al-Sharqiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Zawtar_al-Sharqiyah)

34 people were killed in Lebanon by Israeli strikes on 25 May (according to a tally made in the afternoon and covering the preceding 24-hour period).[^cite_note-moh25may-362][^cite_note-moh24may-363] On 25 May, National Security Minister Itamar Ben-Gvir said the situation in northern Israel, including repeated alerts over Hezbollah drone infiltration, was “unacceptable.” He called for a return to “large-scale combat” and an escalation of military operations in Lebanon.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366] On 26 May and the preceding night, 28 people were reported killed in Israeli strikes (later updated to 34 fatalities),[^cite_note-moh26may-367][^cite_note-moh25may-362] and the number of times Israeli military aircraft flew into Lebanese airspace was notably high.[^cite_note-368]

On the ground, on 26 May, the IDF was clashing with Hezbollah around [Zawtar al-Sharqieh](34789989.md). Apparently, by evening, the IDF set up a position on the outskirts of the village. On 27 May, clashes continued in and around Zawtar al-Sharqieh (but according to reports from the afternoon, the IDF has not established control of the village) and also in the area that lies between Zawtar al-Sharqieh and [Yohmor al-Shaqif](31574515.md).[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370]

On 27 May, an IDF soldier was killed when a drone hit a military site on the Israeli side of the border.[^cite_note-371] Two other soldiers were moderately and/or severely wounded.[^cite_note-372]

The IDF announced an evacuation order, demanding the entire portion of Lebanon that is south of the Zahrani river (which means a larger area than those lands that are south of the Litani river) to be evacuated.

On 27 May and 28 May, daily death tolls from Israeli strikes averaged more than 50 people per day.[^cite_note-28may-373][^cite_note-moh26may-367] On 29 May, the death toll was 31 people.[^cite_note-mohmay29-374][^cite_note-mohmay28-375]

On 28 May, one of the Israeli strikes hit Choueifat in the Beirut metropolitan area, resulting in a total of three deaths: a woman, her child, and another child. One of the children was of Syrian nationality. Israel said this strike was a failed assassination attempt targeting a militant commander.[^cite_note-28may-373]

On 30 May and the preceding night, and on 31 May, Hezbollah fired multiple rockets at northern Israel, although without causing injuries among civilians. Property damage was caused when one of Hezbollah's munitions struck a local shopping mall in Kiryat Shmona while it was closed at night (and because of shabbat),[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] and beachgoers in Nahariya fled the beach as Hezbollah rockets were landing in the sea not far from the coast.[^cite_note-378] Separately, a military base near Beit Hillel in Israel was attacked with a drone; after conflicting reports, the IDF said there were four wounded, all of them soldiers and all of them lightly injured.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380]

During the night of 31 May/1 June, Hezbollah launched two UAVs from Lebanon; one fell near Israeli soldiers in Lebanon while another one crossed into Israel, and sirens were activated in the area surrounding Tiberias, Israel, as this area was thought to be the intended target; the UAV fell without causing civilian injuries.[^cite_note-381]

On 30 May, an IDF soldier was killed near Zawtar al-Sharqieh.[^cite_note-382] The IDF had "pushed past" Zawtar al-Sharqieh and [Mayfadoun](51489221.md) and were now heading toward [Choukine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choukine).[^cite_note-383] They also had occupied Yohmor.[^cite_note-384] The IDF had also managed to reach the outskirts of Nabatieh.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]

On the morning of 31 May, the IDF captured [Beaufort Castle](6853436.md) on a strategic ridge in South Lebanon. The IDF said this was a consequence of an operation that began with a crossing of the Litani a few days earlier; the IDF shared a video of what is said was the first tank that crossed the Litani river.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] Benjamin Netanyahu stated that he instructed the military to "expand its ground manoeuvre in Lebanon".[^cite_note-389] The BBC reported that the IDF appeared to be moving towards the city of Nabatieh and that they repeated their warning to residents that they move north of the [Zahrani River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zahrani_River).[^cite_note-390]

An IDF soldier was killed in Lebanon, in or near Yohmor al-Shaqif and not far from Beaufort Castle, during the night of 31 May/1 June. Another soldier was severely wounded.[^cite_note-391] Later, an IDF soldier was killed and four others moderately and/or severely wounded on 1 June. A lieutenant colonel riding in the same vehicle was lightly wounded.[^cite_note-392]

Daily death tolls on 30 May, 31 May and 1 June were, respectively, 16 people, 41 people, and 21 people.[^cite_note-mohmay30-393][^cite_note-mohmay31-394][^cite_note-mohjune1-395]

As Israel called on residents of Tyre to leave, a group of residents of Tyre issued an appeal to "sav\[e] their city from the ongoing destruction caused by Israeli aggression, which has claimed dozens of its people and seeks to empty it of its residents and remove it from history". A similar appeal issued in Nabatieh was seemingly motivated by the desire to protect Beaufort Castle and other historical heritage. One signatory of the Tyre appeal publicly made the claim that more than half of Tyre has already been destroyed (this did not appear to be true, but healthcare facilities in Tyre including Hiram Hospital, the Italian Hospital and Jabal Amel Hospital were subjected to threatening airstrikes; those near Jabal Amel Hospital leveled a nearby building and damaged the hospital). The Tyre appeal calls for "strengthening the deployment of the Lebanese army and official security forces" in the city and "consolidating the presence of state institutions there in a way that protects residents", just like the similarly worded appeal issued in Nabatieh. These appeals were seen as a challenge against Hezbollah. Both appeals call for declaring the city in question an open city.[^cite_note-396]

### Fourteenth week (1–7 June)

On 1 June, an advisor to Parliament Speaker Berri reported: "The proposal we received \[from the United States] was no Hezbollah attacks on northern Israel and that in return Israel will not bomb Beirut and then gradually the cease-fire will expand to other areas. Speaker Berri's reply was, 'Why a partial cease-fire? Let's have a full cease-fire.'"[^cite_note-397]

Lebanon's embassy in the United States said Hezbollah accepted the US proposal on ["mutual cessation of attacks"](82970933.md).[^cite_note-398]

Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu reportedly held a phone call with US president Donald Trump regarding a planned Israeli strike in Beirut.[^cite_note-399] According to reports, Trump urged Netanyahu to cancel the operation, reportedly calling him "crazy" and stating that "everybody hates Israel because of this".[^cite_note-fuckingcrazy-axios-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] Subsequent reports suggested that the operation was later called off,[^cite_note-fuckingcrazy-axios-400][^cite_note-403] however, Israel continued to strike objectives in Southern Lebanon,[^cite_note-404] with Israel Katz confirming that operations would continue.[^cite_note-405] Hezbollah also continued to fire against Israeli positions in Southern Lebanon and Northern Israel.[^cite_note-406] Following the Israeli strikes Hezbollah stated that they'd not be willing to accept a "partial ceasefire" in which Israel would continue to strike Southern Lebanon, regardless of them refraining from attacking Southern Beirut.[^cite_note-407]

Daily death tolls from Israeli strikes on 2 June, 3 June and 4 June were, respectively, 35 people, 48 people and 10 people.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] An IDF soldier was killed on 4 June. On 5 June, another IDF soldier died in Lebanon of what is suspected to be an accidental firearm discharge; on the same day, an officer was severely wounded while a lieutenant colonel was lightly wounded. Later, an IDF soldier died on 6 June from injuries sustained in a drone attack on the evening of 4 June.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412]

On 3 June an Israeli drone struck a highway connecting Beirut to Southern Lebanon, at the outskirts of the city.[^cite_note-413] Israel said a hostile aircraft had entered its territory, which was later intercepted.[^cite_note-414] On the same day, Israel and Lebanon agreed to renew a ceasefire, mediated by the US, and plan to establish "pilot zones".[^cite_note-415]

On 4 June, Hezbollah rejected the deal that was reached between Lebanese and Israeli authorities in Washington D.C., demanding instead a full ceasefire combined with a full withdrawal of Israeli forces from Lebanon.[^cite_note-416] Berri suggested a simultaneous withdrawal of Hezbollah and Israeli forces from the area south of the Litani, coupled with a full ceasefire.[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418]

On 5 June, Israeli ground troops made another attempt to advance towards [Ghandourieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghandourieh).[^cite_note-419]

A Lebanese soldier and two officers, including one [brigadier general](206220.md), were killed by an Israeli airstrike on their vehicle along the Khardali-Nabatieh road on 6 June.[^cite_note-AJ-06062026-42] The Israeli military stated that the incident is being investigated, and that the vehicle was driving in an area under evacuation orders, which requires coordination, though it did not directly accuse the Lebanese troops of not coordinating with them.[^cite_note-420]

Daily death tolls from Israeli strikes on 5 June and 6 June were, respectively, 32 people and 35 people.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422]

On 7 June, despite the previous agreement as not to strike Southern Beirut, the IDF claimed to have struck the city numerous time, claiming its targets were Hezbollah's sites.[^cite_note-423] Lebanese ⁠state media said that an Israeli strike in southern suburbs of Beirut killed two people and wounded 11 others.[^cite_note-424] The same day, Iran said that American bases and Israeli assets in Middle East are legitimate targets due to the [American naval blockade against the country](82937256.md) and Israeli strikes on [Beirut](37428.md)'s southern suburbs, where neighborhoods that Hezbollah members live in are located.[^cite_note-425] Iran later launched ballistic missiles towards Israel.[^cite_note-426] Israel said that it downed all the missiles.[^cite_note-427] Iran said that it targeted [Ramat David Airbase](5340731.md).[^cite_note-428] Israel said that it will retaliate "forcefully" to the alleged Iranian violation of the truce.[^cite_note-429]

### Fifteenth week (8–14 June)

On 8 June, the IDF announced that it struck military sites in central and western Iran, with explosions being reported in Tehran, Isfahan, and Tabriz; at least one oil/gas facility was also struck.[^cite_note-430] This triggered an exchange of strikes between the two countries. The Houthis also joined the strikes.[^cite_note-431] Iran later said that it had ceased military actions against Israel.[^cite_note-432] Israel later stopped Iran strikes at President Trump's request.[^cite_note-433]

Daily death tolls from Israeli strikes on 7 June, 8 June and 9 June averaged 24 people per day.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] One particular Israeli strike on Tyre on 9 June killed eight people.[^cite_note-437]

On 11 June, the IDF declared they were ready to start an offensive in [Nabatieh](43794798.md), awaiting orders.[^cite_note-438]

On 13 June, the IDF claimed to have killed multiple Hezbollah fighters during attacks on more than 70 targets in southern Lebanon over the past day.[^cite_note-439]

On 14 June, the Lebanese army withdrew from [Kfar Tibnit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Tibnit), and the IDF operated ground activities in the towns and villages surrounding Nabatieh.[^cite_note-440] During a critical phase of the talks about the peace framework, Israeli strikes hit Southern Beirut, drawing American and Iranian condemnation.[^cite_note-441] Israeli forces announced that they killed [Ali Musa Daqduq](12079041.md), a senior Hezbollah commander responsible for the [Karbala provincial headquarters raid](9129813.md) that killed five American soldiers during the [Iraq War](5043324.md) in a strike in southern Lebanon over the weekend.[^cite_note-442]

### Sixteenth week (15–21 June)

On 15 June, Pakistan, the primary mediator, stated that both the United States and Iran declared the "immediate and permanent termination of military operations on all fronts, including in Lebanon" as part of [the agreement](83441543.md) to end the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md). Israel stated that its forces will remain in Lebanon.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] In the afternoon, Hezbollah said that it has not conducted any operations since the announcement of the agreement.[^cite_note-445] One person was killed in an Israeli drone strike on a vehicle in Kfar Tibnit.[^cite_note-446] Displaced people began returning home. Israeli shelling was still happening in some areas. Despite relative calm compared to previous days, there were at least two locations, one in the Tyre District (near Bayada) and another in the Nabatieh District (near Kfar Tibnit, including the [Ali al-Taher](83481817.md) heights) where Israeli forces attempted to expand their hold; after 6 p.m., Hezbollah fired weapons at tanks advancing near Kfar Tibnit, and there were repeated clashes there throughout the night. Israeli shelling hit various other areas overnight.[^cite_note-lorient15june-447][^cite_note-lorient16june-448]

On 16 June, Donald Trump stated that he "was not happy" with how Israel had dealt with the Lebanon War,[^cite_note-449] adding that "If Israel can't do the job without killing everyone else, Syria should do the job" in a joint military venture with the United States. He also added that this had already been suggested in the past to both Israel and Syria.[^cite_note-450] Israeli strikes continued, including in Nabatieh and surrounding areas.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452]

On 17 June, Israel attempted to capture Ali al-Taher hill, a strategic hill southeast of Nabatieh, with Hezbollah claiming to be in active combat and to have repelled various Israeli offensives in the area.[^cite_note-453] Reports suggested the clashes were fierce.[^cite_note-454] Following the failure of Israel to capture the hill, Hezbollah stated it destroyed three Merkava tanks and targeted troops with rocket and artillery fire.[^cite_note-455]

An Israeli soldier was killed in Lebanon, and a colonel and a lieutenant colonel were both moderately wounded, with the IDF stating it occurred on 17 June.[^cite_note-456]

On 18 June Israel released a map showing allegedly occupied territories in Southern Lebanon, which included most of the territories under the Yellow Line, and additionally, Kfar Tibnit and Ali al-Taher.[^cite_note-457]
