During the first half of 19 June and the preceding night, Israel struck numerous targets in Southern Lebanon; the death toll from Israeli strikes over a 24-hour period was 68, later updated to 83.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-lorient20june-460] Hezbollah struck Israeli positions within Kfar Tebnit.[^cite_note-toitank-461] US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) declared that a ceasefire mediated by the US, Qatar and Iran had been implemented between Hezbollah and Israel starting from 4 p.m. local time.[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463] Four IDF soldiers were reported killed, including a lieutenant colonel, with the IDF saying it occurred during fighting against Hezbollah in Kfar Tebnit last night.[^cite_note-toitank-461][^cite_note-464] Media attributed the postponing of the physical signature of the Islamabad Memorandum in [Switzerland](26748.md) to the clashes between Israel and Hezbollah that happened during the day.[^cite_note-465] Despite the ceasefire Israel continued to strike Southern Lebanon numerous times after 4 pm.[^cite_note-466]

On 20 June, Israel struck numerous locations in Lebanon, saying they were Hezbollah targets. The death toll from Israeli strikes increased by 145 people over two days.[^cite_note-lorient20june-460] Hezbollah fired, many times, at Israeli forces stationed in Southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-467] Al Jazeera called the day "devastating".[^cite_note-468] The IDF announced that a soldier was killed in Lebanon.[^cite_note-469] On the same day, Iran declared that it closed the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md) again due to Israeli strikes in Lebanon, describing them as a violation of [its deal](83441543.md) with the US. This claim was denied by the US military.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471]

On 21 June, Benjamin Netanyahu and Israel Katz, ordered the IDF to stay put in the occupied Lebanese territories and to hold fire everywhere aside from Ali al-Taher Hill, where the IDF and Hezbollah have been involved in numerous clashes.[^cite_note-472]

### Seventeenth week (22–28 June)

On 24 June, the IDF reported that the [Ali al-Taher](83481817.md) ridge contained a Hezbollah [underground facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underground_facility) built with Iranian assistance and that dozens of fighters are trapped in.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474]

On 26 June, US secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) announced a framework deal between Israel and Lebanon that aims to achieve "lasting peace and security" through US mediation. In the framework, Lebanon recognizes the legitimacy of Israel as a state, wishes to "formally conclude any state of war" between Lebanon and Israel, and commits to full disarmament of Hezbollah and dismantling of its infrastructure. The framework states that after full disarmament of Hezbollah and dismantling of its infrastructure is achieved, it will "enable" the IDF to progressively withdraw from Lebanon. This framework was rejected by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477]

On 28 June, Israel said its military had destroyed a Hezbollah tunnel in [Majdal Zoun](78216314.md), stating that the 200 metres (660 ft) tunnel contained weapons and rocket launcher shafts.[^cite_note-478]

### Eighteenth week (29 June – 5 July)

Attacks on Lebanon continued following the peace framework.[^cite_note-479]

On 30 June, Iran said that Iran, the US, and Lebanon have agreed to form a committee to oversee the end of the war.[^cite_note-480]

On 2 July, the [Egoz commando](4311894.md) unit of the IDF claimed to have killed a Hezbollah fighter exiting a tunnel in the Ali al-Taher ridge area.[^cite_note-481]

### July

Despite Israel and Lebanon pursuing talks, Israel continued to strike locations in Southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-482]

On 15 July, the [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) stated that Israel agreed to withdraw from pilot zones in Lebanon's south.[^cite_note-483] Shortly after, Israeli military said that it killed three Hezbollah members in the area of [Beit Yahoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Yahoun).[^cite_note-484]

On 20 July, the US announced that Israel handed over sections of the buffer zone occupied by it in southern Lebanon to the [Lebanese government](78886120.md) as per [its U.S.-mediated deal with Beirut](82970933.md).[^cite_note-485]

On 21 July, Lebanon announced that Lebanese army started deploying in [Zawtar al-Gharbiyeh](78327596.md) following Israeli withdrawal from the town in accordance with [a US mediated agreement](82970933.md).[^cite_note-486]

### August

On 5 August, the IDF reported the deaths of two soldiers, one of them holding the rank of major (res.)[^cite_note-487] Later in the day, Israel carried out retaliatory attacks that left one dead and twelve wounded in the village of [Tibnin](6787186.md).[^cite_note-488]

On 7 August, Lebanon and Israel agreed on a shortlist of states that could send forces to verify Hezbollah disarmament.[^cite_note-489]

On 8 August, IDF forces re-entered Zawtar al-Gharbiyah in violation of the U.S.-brokered agreement.[^cite_note-Zawtar_20260808-490] Separately, the Lebanese military reported that three soldiers where wounded disarming an IED in Zawtar al-Gharbiyah.[^cite_note-Zawtar_20260808-490]

On 11 August, an Israeli drone strike hit vehicles belonging to the [Islamic Health Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Health_Authority?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Islamic Message Scouts Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Message_Scouts_Association?action=edit\&redlink=1) on the outskirts of [Nabatieh](43794798.md).[^cite_note-491]

On 12 August Israel performed strikes on Nabatieh and [Al-Mansouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mansouri) in violation of the U.S.-brokered agreement and accidentally targeted IDF soldiers with an interceptor rocket with no casualties.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]

On 15 August Hezbollah performed a drone strike on IDF forces at the Ali Taher Ridge area seriously wounding an officer and two soldiers from the [Yahalom](745562.md) combat engineering unit.[^cite_note-494]

On 19 August Israel performed retaliatory strikes on the Ali Taher Ridge and Kfar Rumman.[^cite_note-495]

On 20 August Israel performed both air and artillery strikes on Houla, Markaba, Wadi Slouqi, Qabrikha, al-Mansouri, Majdalzoun, Ali al-Taher and Touline while the IDF performed demolitions in Zawtar al-Sharqiyeh, Majdalzoun, al-Mansouri and al-Khiam, also in retaliation for the 15 August strike.[^cite_note-496]

During the night of 22 August into the early morning of 23 August Israel launched significant areal bombardments of the Ali Taher Ridge and surrounding woods and villages with a military spokesmen stating that they "targeted vehicles approaching Ali Taher or traveling nearby" followed by strikes on the villages of Zawtar al-Sharqieh and Yohmor al-Shaqif near Nabatieh while the IDF demolished homes in Kfar Tebnit and Arnoun.[^cite_note-497]

On 24 August IDF forces advanced beyond the 'yellow line' established by the U.S.-brokered agreement and entered the town of Haris with bulldozers while the IDF continued demolitions in Khiam, Taybeh, Mays Al Jebel and Yohmor.[^cite_note-498]

On 25 August the IDF attacked Tel al-Dabsha, Dahiyat Kafr Rumman and the al-Deir neighborhood in al-Nabatiyeh al-Fawqa to further isolate the Ali Taher Ridge.[^cite_note-499] Additional strikes where also reported near Mansouri.[^cite_note-500]

On 26 August Hezbollah launched two more drone strikes on IDF personnel near the Ali Taher Ridge with the IDF shooting down one, while the other caused no casualties.[^cite_note-501]

On 27 August the IDF performed retaliatory strikes around Nabatieh that included a strike on [Arabsalim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabsalim) that killed a woman who had been married just 48 hours prior.[^cite_note-502]

### September

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_Lebanon_war\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_Lebanon_war\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3A2026_Lebanon_war\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=2026+Lebanon+war). *(October 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

On 3 September, the Israeli government announced it had [taken control of Ali Taher hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Ali_al-Taher) and had gained "operational control" of the ridge, with [Defense Minister](3226446.md) [Israel Katz](19041378.md) stating that the IDF was working to destroy the network of tunnels underneath it to stop future reuse by Hezbollah and that Israeli control of the ridge was part of a "security zone in southern Lebanon" that it was establishing.[^cite_note-503] In the days after the announcement, a visiting [Associated Press](18935732.md) photographer was unable to find any IDF presence on the hill. Hezbollah legislator Ihab Hamadeh expressed doubt, noting that Israel would not need to continually attack the hill if it already controlled it.[^cite_note-\:1-504] On 10 September 2026, the IDF said that it had detonated more than 1,000 tons of TNT (4,200 GJ) of explosives, destroying the network of tunnels stretching 2 kilometres (1.2 mi) underneath the ridge and setting off tremors that measured 4.1 on the [Richter scale](19467352.md) in what Lebanon's [National News Agency](73575882.md) called a "massive explosion".[^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507]

Hezbollah has consistently rejected handing over control of Ali Taher to the Lebanese military out of concern that Israel would take it and use it as a bargaining chip to take control of more territory.[^cite_note-\:1-504]

Seven people were killed on September 5. The next day, an Israeli strike on a residential building in the southern Lebanese town of [Kfar Reman](51521761.md) killed 12 members of the same family, including at least two children and four women. Another strike killed a paramedic.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509][^cite_note-510]

### October

On 3 October the IDF targeted a private school in [Houla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houla) with demolition charges while several homes where also burned. Israeli shelling was also reported in Mansouri, Majdal Zoun, [Khaim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaim?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Tallouseh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallouseh), [Mayfadoun](51489221.md) and [Zawtar al-Sharqiyah](34789989.md) while airstrikes where reported in [Bani Hayyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bani_Hayyan) and [Kfar Tibni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Tibni?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-511]

## Economic targets

Israel's efforts against Hezbollah have focused on its military assets as well as its finances, with an emphasis on the estimated \$700 million directed from Iran each year through intermediaries that accounted for most of Hezbollah's annual operating budget. Some of the earliest Israeli military efforts in March 2026 targeted the organization's supply of gold and foreign currency, as well as branches of the [Al-Qard Al-Hasan Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qard_Al-Hasan_Association), a non-profit financial institution that has been described as being "Hezbollah's bank in Lebanon". Money changing offices and gas stations were also struck, all as part of an effort to squeeze the organization's sources of funding.[^cite_note-512]

## Diplomatic efforts

Further information: [2026 Israel–Lebanon peace talks](82916672.md)

On 9 April, it was reported that a round of negotiations between Israel and Lebanon has been scheduled to take place in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), United States. The talks will involve the US ambassador to Lebanon [Michel Issa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Issa), Israeli ambassador to the US [Yechiel Leiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yechiel_Leiter), and Lebanese ambassador to the US [Nada Hamadeh Moawad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nada_Hamadeh_Moawad). However, Israeli officials rejected a ceasefire with Hezbollah in Lebanon ahead of the talks, which was confirmed on 10 April by Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu.[^cite_note-513] On 12 April, Lebanese prime minister Nawaf Salam said that his government is "working to end this war, secure the Israeli withdrawal from all our territory, and secure the return of all our prisoners."[^cite_note-514] The first talks between the two governments in decades occurred on 14 April in the presence of secretary of state [Marco Rubio](5502549.md).[^cite_note-NPR-14042026-265]

As US efforts to convince the Israeli government to refrain from further attacks in Lebanon failed, the governments of the US, Iran and Pakistan agreed on 21 June to create a joint mechanism of consultation in order to resolve this issue.[^cite_note-515]

On 26 June, a direct framework agreement between Israel and Lebanon was signed in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) in order to obtain Israeli withdrawal from all parts of Lebanon, to be carried out in parallel with the dismantling of Hezbollah through the efforts of the Lebanese army.[^cite_note-516] Israel stated that this agreement superseded the prior discussions involving Iran.[^cite_note-517]

## Impact and casualties

### Casualties

As of 20 June, at least 4,057 people have been killed and 12,121 wounded by Israeli strikes in Lebanon during the war.[^cite_note-Lebanon1-64] Internal Hezbollah sources said that over 1,000 Hezbollah fighters had been killed in the war[^cite_note-Hezbollah1-21] while the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) said that it had killed 1,700 fighters.[^cite_note-Hezbollah2-518]



Funerary procession in Tyre for a deceased Hezbollah fighter, 21 April 2026

On 2 March, the first day of the war, [Lebanon's Health Ministry](77943319.md) reported that at least 31 people had been killed and 149 injured in the initial Israeli strikes, including 20 killed and 91 wounded in Beirut's southern suburbs and 11 killed and 58 wounded in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-L'Orient-90] Civilian displacement became significant, with families fleeing southern areas toward cities like [Sidon](28366.md).[^cite_note-Ravid-519] In the afternoon, the IDF announced that Hussein Makled, head of Hezbollah's intelligence headquarters, had been killed in overnight strikes in Beirut. Local media also reported the death of the brother of the late [Mohammad Hussein Fadlallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Hussein_Fadlallah) along with his wife as a result of strikes in [Haret Hreik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haret_Hreik).[^cite_note-520] [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) in Lebanon announced that its commander, Adham Adnan al-Othman, had been killed in Beirut's southern suburbs.[^cite_note-lot-jihad-95] At night, casualties rose to 52 deaths and 154 injuries, according to the Health Ministry.[^cite_note-L'Orient-90]

On 8 April, shortly after the United States and Iran agreed to [a two-week ceasefire](82888027.md) and Hezbollah signaled that it would pause its attacks on Israel accordingly,[^cite_note-521] Israel [launched what it described as its "most powerful attacks" on Lebanon](82903314.md), killing at least 357 people.[^cite_note-AJ357-522][^cite_note-ha-523] The casualty count was among the highest of the war.[^cite_note-ha-523][^cite_note-Reuters-08042026-248]

The percentage of women and children among those killed, overall lower than in the [Gaza War](74998519.md), changed over time. During the first twenty days of the war, 1 out of 9 people killed by Israeli actions in Lebanon was a child.[^cite_note-524] The percentage of children among those killed decreased during the later stages of the war.

### Displacement

According to Lebanon, over 1.2 million Lebanese nationals,[^cite_note-displaced-65] corresponding to more than one-sixth of the country's population,[^cite_note-525] were displaced.[^cite_note-Kelliher-526][^cite_note-AP_News-527] Israel used heavy machinery to destroy Lebanese villages and civilian infrastructure south of the Litani River in an effort to prevent the return of civilians.[^cite_note-528] Over 40,000 homes in southern Lebanon have been destroyed.[^cite_note-NPR-14042026-265] Israel's evacuation orders in Lebanon covered an area of over 1,470 square kilometres (570 sq mi), encompassing 14% of the country's territory. The displacement in Lebanon has been called one of the most severe and fastest-growing refugee crises worldwide.[^cite_note-displaced-65] The war exacerbated the economic and humanitarian problems that have been ongoing in Lebanon since the [2019 financial crisis](63804918.md) and [2020 Beirut explosion](64752725.md).[^cite_note-ForeignPolicy-03042026-529] In May 2026, [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that Israeli issued evacuation orders "span about 2,000 km<sup>2</sup> of Lebanon – about a fifth of the entire country".[^cite_note-530]

The number of displaced people remained around 500,000 as of early July 2026.[^cite_note-531]

### Demolitions

Multiple Lebanese villages adjacent to the border were already destroyed by Israel in 2024 by shelling and controlled demolitions.[^cite_note-532]

During the 2026 war, Israel conducted a large-scale campaign of controlled demolitions of homes and other civilian buildings, including in larger towns. The central part of Bint Jbeil was completely razed, mostly during April 2026.[^cite_note-533] According to a study by [Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde) between February 28 and April 29 2026, 45% of urban areas located in the Yellow Line are destroyed or damaged by the Israeli army.[^cite_note-534][^cite_note-535]

According to the [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md), “The issuance of blanket evacuation orders, combined with the destruction of urban and village housing that displaced persons would have returned to, is consistent with the pattern of [domicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domicide) that was initiated during the [genocide in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_in_Gaza).” [^cite_note-536]

### Return migration of Syrian refugees

See also: [Return of refugees of the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Return_of_refugees_of_the_Syrian_civil_war)

According to the [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_United_Nations_High_Commissioner_for_Refugees), the total population of Syrian refugees in the entirety of Lebanon was 1.4 million people in March.[^cite_note-537] Due to the war, thousands of individuals of Syrian heritage migrated back to Syria, especially by using the Joussieh border crossing.[^cite_note-538] According to a 13 March report by [Intersos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersos), other local agencies had recorded as of 10 March that 85,000 to 90,000 people had crossed the main legal border crossings from Lebanon to Syria. Demographically, these refugees comprised 93% Syrian returnees and 7% Lebanese citizens.[^cite_note-539] More than 125,000 people had crossed the border from Lebanon into Syria by 18 March;[^cite_note-540] according to the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees's Displacement Tracking Matrix, 119,000 of them were Syrians.[^cite_note-541] By 31 March 2026 up to 200,000 people had crossed the Lebanese border into Syria, around 180,000 of which being Syrians and 28,000 Lebanese.[^cite_note-542] By 2 May 2026 the total amount of Syrians that had crossed the Lebanese border into Syria were 306,888 alongside 64,865 Lebanese.[^cite_note-543] In August 2026, Lebanese [Minister of Interior and Municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_and_Municipalities_\(Lebanon\)) [Ahmad al-Hajjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_al-Hajjar) stated that out of the 1.4 million former Syrian refugee population around 650,000 refugees had been removed from the UNHCR database after their return to Syria, adding that "measures facilitating the voluntary return of Syrian refugees" by the Lebanese government had been extended to up until 30 September 2026.[^cite_note-544]

## Reactions

### Lebanese government

On 2 March 2026, Lebanese prime minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) condemned Hezbollah's rocket and drone strikes from southern Lebanon, calling them irresponsible acts outside the authority of the Lebanese state that endanger national security. While he emphasized that all military action must fall under the government's control, he did not formally declare Hezbollah outlawed, focusing instead on preventing unauthorized armed operations.[^cite_note-545][^cite_note-546]

Following an emergency Cabinet meeting convened early in the day amid the escalating strikes, Salam announced a [total ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Lebanese_legal_actions_against_Hezbollah?action=edit\&redlink=1) on all military activities by [Hezbollah](13919.md), demanding the group [surrender its weapons to the state and restrict itself to political activities only](80353105.md). He affirmed that decisions on war and peace rest exclusively with the state, rejecting any unauthorized actions from Lebanese territory, and called on security forces to prevent such violations and arrest those responsible.[^cite_note-L'Orient-90]

The [National News Agency](73575882.md) reported that justice minister [Adel Nassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adel_Nassar) requested the public prosecutor at the [Court of Cassation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Cassation), Jamal Hajjar, and the government commissioner at the Military Court to assign the security forces with immediately arresting those involved in launching rockets from southern Lebanon toward Israel.[^cite_note-L'Orient-90]

The Lebanese government announced on 5 March it would "arrest and repatriate" anyone in Lebanon connected to Iran's Revolutionary Guards, leading to the departure of dozens of Iranian officers from Beirut in the past two days, according to [Axios](52436164.md) and several other sources. Most of those departing are believed to be members of the [Quds Force](4028355.md) who had been serving as military advisers to Hezbollah and influencing its operations.[^cite_note-547]

Following the Lebanese [legal actions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Lebanese_legal_actions_against_Hezbollah?action=edit\&redlink=1) against Hezbollah and the IRGC, more than 150 Iranian nationals left Lebanon, including diplomats.[^cite_note-548]

President Joseph Aoun said the state is the only one that can protect its citizens and that no one expected that the country once again to become the arena for the wars of others.[^cite_note-549] Minister of Information Paul Morkos has ordered all media (television, radio and the national news agency) to stop using the word "resistance" affiliated to Hezbollah, meaning they are not to call them by that name anymore.[^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551]

On 9 March 2026, the [Parliament of Lebanon](1618242.md) postponed [parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2028_Lebanese_general_election) from May 2026 to May 2028.[^cite_note-552][^cite_note-553]

In a 22 March 2026 interview to [Saudi Arabia](349303.md)'s [Al-Hadath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hadath) television station, Salam said the Iranian IRGC is directing Hezbollah in its war on Israel and that this war was imposed on Lebanon. "These were the Revolutionary Guards, who are present and, unfortunately, managing military operations in Lebanon ...These people have fake passports and entered the country illegally". He mentioned again the government's unprecedented decision to ban Hezbollah's military actions, calling them to disarm and hand their weapons to the state.[^cite_note-554]

On 24 March 2026, the Lebanese government declared it was [expelling](521241.md) the Iranian ambassador, [Mohammad Reza Raouf Sheibani](82044151.md), in response to Iran's continued interference in Lebanon's internal affairs.[^cite_note-555]

### Hezbollah

Hezbollah condemned the Lebanese government after it banned Hezbollah's military and security activities, stating "We understand the Lebanese government's impotence in the face of the brutal Zionist enemy, which violates national sovereignty, occupies land, and poses a continuous threat to the country's security and stability", and that it is the government's right "to decide on war and peace", concluding that "given this clear weakness and deficiency, we see no justification for Prime Minister Salam and his government to take such aggressive measures against the Lebanese who reject the occupation."[^cite_note-556] Hezbollah later said that its attack was a "defensive act" after over a year of Israeli attacks despite a truce. It added that "for fifteen months, Israeli aggression against Lebanon has continued through killing, destruction, bulldozing, and all forms of criminal acts."[^cite_note-NPA-557]

Group members have openly threatened to topple the government. [Wafiq Safa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wafiq_Safa) vowed that Hezbollah would force the government to retract its ban on their military activities "regardless of the method". He further threatened a "different agenda" after the war, including a potential return to the street-level violence seen in the [May 2008 clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Lebanon_conflict).[^cite_note-558] The deputy head of Hezbollah's political council, [Mahmoud Qamati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud_Qamati), warned that the group is "capable of turning the country and government upside down". He explicitly labeled the government "traitors" and "complicit," comparing them to the Vichy regime and stating that a post-war confrontation is "inevitable".[^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560] Hezbollah official [Nawwaf Moussawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawwaf_Moussawi), compared Aoun to [Anwar Sadat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Sadat) who was a former Egyptian president that signed a peace agreement with Israel at the [Camp David Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David_Accords) resulting in his [assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Anwar_Sadat) in 1981, adding that any peace deal with Israel would be "rejected, unrecognized and thrown in the trash, like the [May 17, 1983 agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_17_Agreement)."[^cite_note-561]

### Other domestic political parties

Many of Lebanon's other political parties have expressed dismay with Hezbollah's actions and have urged the Lebanese government to disarm the party.

[Samir Geagea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samir_Geagea), chairman of the [LF](2113218.md), has been the most prominent critic, calling Hezbollah's demands for the government to retract its decisions "completely unacceptable". He accused the group of taking the state hostage for 40 years and dragging Lebanon into an "insane" war to serve Iranian interests rather than national ones.[^cite_note-562] The Kataeb Party leader Samy Gemayel stated that Hezbollah's "Sick mindset" dragged Lebanon into a "free suicide" operation.[^cite_note-563]

Beirut MP [Fouad Makhzoumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Makhzoumi) called for a total ban on Hezbollah activities stressing state monopoly on arms and advocated the deployment of the army to the southern suburbs.[^cite_note-564][^cite_note-565]

In a meeting between Lebanese Christian representatives and [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md), the [Lebanese Forces](2113218.md) party went as far as to suggest rearming its [party militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Forces_\(militia\)) to make up for the Lebanese government's perceived inaction in disarmament.[^cite_note-566]

### Israel

The IDF said that it would "not allow \[Hezbollah] to pose a threat to the State of Israel and harm the residents of the north", further accusing it of "destroying the state of Lebanon."[^cite_note-Silkoff-567] IDF spokesperson [Effie Defrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effie_Defrin) said "Hezbollah opened fire last night. We warned it. It will pay a heavy price." When asked about a possible ground incursion into Lebanon, Defrin stated "all options are on the table."[^cite_note-568] Israel also indirectly threatened to strike Lebanese government targets unless it intervened against Hezbollah. Lebanese foreign minister [Youssef Rajji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youssef_Rajji) said that Lebanon had "received warnings indicating that any intervention on the part of Hezbollah could prompt Israel to strike infrastructure targets."[^cite_note-569]

### International

See also: [2026 Michigan synagogue attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Michigan_synagogue_attack)

[France](5843419.md) condemned Hezbollah's attacks against Israel as "irresponsible" and expressed its solidarity "in the face of the ordeal Lebanon is going through due to the irresponsible decision of Hezbollah". It also expressed solidarity with Israeli civilians affected by the Iranian attacks.[^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571]

Syrian president [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md) said that he supports the Lebanese government's efforts to [disarm Hezbollah](80353105.md).[^cite_note-572] Syria also warned that it would respond to any attack on its territory, after accusing Hezbollah of targeting Syrian Army positions and moving forces to the border.[^cite_note-Iwaza-2026-17]

United States president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) said "Hezbollah is a big problem" and "they're rapidly being eliminated" in response to Israel's ground offensive in southern Lebanon, adding that he spoke to Israeli leaders about the offensive.[^cite_note-573] US ambassador to the United Nations [Mike Waltz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Waltz) said that the US strongly condemns attacks by Hezbollah against Israel and supports Israel's right to defend itself. He further described Hezbollah as an Iranian proxy that does not represent the interests of the Lebanese people and commended the Lebanese government's decision to ban its military activities.[^cite_note-574]

[Spain](26667.md) "strongly condemned" the Israeli attacks on Lebanon, and urged full compliance with [UN Security Council Resolution 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_1701).[^cite_note-575] [Turkey](11125639.md) also condemned the Israeli ground operation in Lebanon, calling it one of the "genocidal and collective punishment policies, this time in Lebanon," of the [Netanyahu government](72545811.md), and warned against "another humanitarian catastrophe."[^cite_note-576]

In a joint statement, France, [Canada](5042916.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), and the [United Kingdom](31717.md) expressed that they were "gravely concerned" at the violence in Lebanon and called for "immediate de-escalation." They jointly condemned Hezbollah's "decision to join Iran in hostilities" and also called for the proposed Israeli ground invasion of Lebanon to "be averted" as it could trigger "devastating humanitarian consequences and could lead to a protracted conflict."[^cite_note-577] Canadian prime minister [Mark Carney](13571049.md) condemned what he called the "illegal invasion" of Lebanon by Israel, calling it "a violation of \[Lebanon's] territorial sovereignty" and noting that the Lebanese government has made efforts to ban Hezbollah's activities.[^cite_note-578]

[Brazil](3383.md) condemned [8 April 2026 Israeli attacks in Lebanon](82903314.md) as a breach of the April 2026 ceasefire, demanding that Israel suspend military operations and respect Lebanon's territorial integrity.[^cite_note-579] [Russia](25391.md) condemned Israel's post-ceasefire strikes in Lebanon,[^cite_note-580] as well as attacks on journalists and UNIFIL peacekeepers, and called on Israel to "abandon the use of force alone to address its legitimate security concerns, withdraw its military units from occupied territories, and return to the path of diplomacy."[^cite_note-581] Following Israel's 8 April strikes, [China](5405.md)'s [Foreign Ministry](7453497.md) issued a statement that Lebanon's sovereignty should not be violated.[^cite_note-582]

Ahead of peace talks in Islamabad between US and Iran on the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), [Pakistan](23235.md) opposed Israel's military actions in Lebanon, stating that the terms of truce between Iran and the US and Israel included Lebanon. Both Tel Aviv and Washington denied Pakistan's claims. Pakistan's Defence Minister [Khawaja Asif](16345137.md) reacted by labelling Israel as "evil" and a "curse for humanity." Netanyahu's office responded by stating that such remarks amounted to calling for Israel's destruction and called Asif's statements "outrageous." "This is not a statement that can be tolerated from any government, especially not from one that claims to be a neutral arbiter for peace," the statement read.[^cite_note-Anadolu-20260410-583]
